# Signed tensor densities and diagram budgets for the Thorp shuffle

Coordinate `EN-115-000-0003-2-2026-PR-T4-UK-EX-M2-B-ML-M-0496` | `PM-238.10` | status ACTIVE | original slug `Signed-tensor-densities-and-diagram-budgets-for-coordinate-sweeps-September-26-2026` | date 2026-09-26 | derived from the openai/math preprint Signed-tensor-densities-and-diagram-budgets-for-coordinate-sweeps-September-26-2026, modified (Apache-2.0). Publication status: DRAFT.

<!-- PM:BEGIN pm.node.v2 -->
```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove that a fixed number of coordinate sweeps of the Thorp shuffle on $`2^d`$ cards makes the squared <i>L</i><sup>2</sup> distance of the full permutation density from uniform tend to zero as $`d\\to\\infty`$. In particular, the total-variation mixing time is $`\\Theta(d)`$ physical shuffles.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that a fixed number of coordinate sweeps of the Thorp shuffle on $`2^d`$ cards makes the squared <i>L</i><sup>2</sup> distance of the full permutation density from uniform tend to zero as $`d\to\infty`$. In particular, the total-variation mixing time is $`\Theta(d)`$ physical shuffles.

- **claim-spin-moment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-s-spin-moment)): For each sufficiently small fixed $\bar\eta>0$, choose a sufficiently small $\bar\kappa>0$ with $\bar\kappa<\bar\eta/256$. There is an integer $r\ge1$, depending only on these fixed constants, such that for every $d\ge1$, every $\lambda\vdash n=2^d$, and every occurrence <a href="#ten:spin:eq:1" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ten:spin:eq:1">[ten:spin:eq:1]</a>, $$\label{ten:spin:eq:3} \log\!\left(D_\lambda\operatorname{Tr}_{V_\lambda}(B_n^*B_n)^r\right) \le\eta_d F(\alpha,\beta)+g_n(l),$$ where $$\eta_d=\bar\eta\left(1-\frac1{2\sqrt d}\right),\qquad \kappa_d=\bar\kappa\left(1-\frac1{2\sqrt d}\right),\qquad g_n(l)=\max\{0,\kappa_d l\log n-l\log(n/l)\}.$$ Set $g_n(0)=0$ and interpret the logarithm of a zero trace as $-\infty$.
  - Explanation: Every prescribed signed occurrence in a Specht module has a bounded weighted moment of the sweep operator, controlled by the entropy of the part lists and a clipped saving for the remainder marks.
- **claim-full-deck-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-spin-full-deck)): There is an absolute positive integer $L$ such that $$\mathbb E_{U_{S_n}}\|f_{d,L}-1\|^2\longrightarrow0 \qquad(d\longrightarrow\infty).$$ The convergence is uniform over starting orders. Moreover $t_{\mathrm{mix}}(d)=\Theta(d)$.
  - Explanation: A fixed number of sweeps drives the squared L2 distance of the permutation density to zero, so the physical mixing time has the order of d.
- **claim-annihilation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-annihilation)): The sign representation is annihilated by every nonempty fair layer. Every irreducible of $S_n$ indexed by a shape with more than $n/2$ rows is annihilated by a sweep.
  - Explanation: The sign representation and all shapes with too many rows are killed by a sweep.
- **claim-finite-gap** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finitegap)): For every fixed dyadic $n\ge2$, $\\|B_n(\lambda)\\|_{\mathrm{op}}<1$ on every nontrivial irreducible.
  - Explanation: For each fixed dyadic size the sweep is a strict contraction on every nontrivial irreducible.
- **claim-support-obstruction** (bound, [source](#stmt-ten-support)): For every integer $t\ge0$, $$\\|q_d^{*t}-U_{S_n}\\|_{\mathrm{TV}}\ge1-2^{tn/2}/n!.$$ Consequently $t_{\mathrm{mix}}(d)\ge\lceil(2/n)\log_2(3n!/4)\rceil=2d-O(1)$.
  - Explanation: Counting the available coin strings bounds the total variation distance from below and yields a lower bound on mixing time of order d.
- **claim-schatten-powers** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-schatten)): For integers $r\ge s\ge1$ and any matrix $T$, setting $Q=T^*T$, $$\\|T^r\\|_{\mathrm{HS}}^2\le\\|Q\\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{r-s}\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}Q^s.$$
  - Explanation: The Hilbert-Schmidt norm of a power of a nonnormal matrix is bounded through its operator norm and a trace of powers of its positive square.
- **claim-degree-sums** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-degrees)): Uniformly in $n$, $\sum_{\lambda\vdash n}D_\lambda^{-1}$ is bounded, and $$\sum_{\lambda\ne(n),(1^n)}D_\lambda^{-1}\longrightarrow0.$$ If $\ell=n-\max(\lambda_1,\lambda'_1)$, then $$\log D_\lambda\ge(\log2)\sqrt\ell/2, \qquad \sup_n\sum_{\lambda\vdash n}D_\lambda^{-32}<\infty.$$
  - Explanation: Inverse irreducible dimensions are summable uniformly, with the nontrivial part vanishing, and dimensions grow with the distance from a one-row or one-column shape.
- **claim-signed-type-density** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-signed-type-density)): For every such $(a,b)$ there is a probability distribution on even densities $R$ such that $$Q_{a,b}\preceq e^{F(a,b)}u(a,b)\,\mathbb E R^{\otimes p}. \label{eq:signed-type-density-exact}$$ For every even density $T$ and every type $(a,b)$, $$\\|T^{\otimes p}Q_{a,b}\\|_{\rm op}\le e^{-F(a,b)}. \label{eq:signed-global-density-upper}$$ Moreover $u(a,b)\le(p+1)^{q(q-1)}$.* *For arbitrary parity dimensions, the upper bound has the following carrier form. Let $E_+=\mathbb C^{r_+}$ and $E_-=\mathbb C^{r_-}$, for nonnegative integers $r_+,r_-$, and let $a,b$ have at most $r_+,r_-$ parts, respectively, with $\|a\|+\|b\|=p$. Write $\mathbf S_a(E_+)$ and $\mathbf S_b(E_-)$ for the ordinary polynomial carriers of highest weights $a,b$. For a positive parity-preserving matrix $T$ on $E_+\oplus E_-$, let its induced carrier action be $\pi_{a,b}(T)=\mathbf S_a(T\|_{E_+})\otimes\mathbf S_b(T\|_{E_-})$. With $F(a,b)$ given by the same entropy formula, $$\\|\pi_{a,b}(T)\\|_{\rm op} \le (\operatorname{Tr}T)^p e^{-F(a,b)}. \label{eq:signed-carrier-density-upper}$$ A zero-dimensional factor has the empty partition and contributes the scalar identity. If $p=0$, then $\pi_{a,b}(T)=1$ and the right-hand side is interpreted as one, even when $\operatorname{Tr}T=0$.
  - Explanation: A signed type projection is dominated by an average of tensor powers of even densities, with an entropy and carrier-dimension factor, plus a matching operator-norm bound.
- **claim-one-sided-angle** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-one-sided-type-angle)): Let $W$ be any set of $p=sm-l$ occupied cells of an $s$-row, $m$-column rectangle. On $V^{\otimes W}$, let $P$ be the global $G_q$-type projection with type $(\alpha,\beta)$, and let $P_H$ and $P_K$ be products of prescribed column and row $G_q$-type projections. Write $(a^j,b^j)$ for the column types and $(c^i,d^i)$ for the row types, with sizes equal to their respective occupied block sizes. Put $$\begin{gathered} F=F(\alpha,\beta),\qquad F_H=\sum_jF(a^j,b^j),\qquad F_K=\sum_iF(c^i,d^i),\\ U_H=\prod_ju(a^j,b^j),\qquad U_K=\prod_iu(c^i,d^i). \end{gathered}$$ If $b_i$ cells are missing from row $i$, put $$\Phi_K(W)=\prod_{i:b_i<m} \left(\frac m{m-b_i}\right)^{m-b_i},$$ using factor one when a row is empty. Then $$\\|P_H P_KP\\|_{\rm op}^2 \le\min\{1,e^{F_H+F_K-F}U_HU_K\Phi_K(W)\} \le\min\{1,e^{F_H+F_K-F+l}U_HU_K\}. \label{eq:one-sided-type-angle-exact}$$ In particular $U_HU_K\le(p+1)^{(s+m)q(q-1)}$.
  - Explanation: The squared norm of the product of column, row and global type projections on a board with missing cells is controlled by entropy differences and a missing-cell factor.
- **claim-positive-restriction** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-pointwise-positive-restriction)): Let $G$ be finite, let $H\le G$, and let $x=\sum_{g\in G}x_g g$ be positive in $\mathbb C[G]$. Set $E_Hx=\sum_{h\in H}x_hh$. For $\rho\in\widehat H$ let $\Lambda_\rho$ be the set of $\mu\in\widehat G$ whose restriction to $H$ contains $\rho$. With $d_\rho=\dim\rho$ and $d_\mu=\dim\mu$, $$0\le\operatorname{Tr}_\rho(E_Hx) \le\frac1{[G:H]d_\rho} \sum_{\mu\in\Lambda_\rho}d_\mu\operatorname{Tr}_\mu(x). \label{eq:pointwise-positive-restriction}$$ For $G=S_s$ and $H=S_{s-h}$ fixing $h$ specified points, the index is $(s)_h=s!/(s-h)!$.
  - Explanation: Restricting a positive group-algebra element to a subgroup keeps positivity and bounds the trace by the index-scaled ambient traces.
- **claim-hook-carriers** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-signed-hook-carriers)): Fix $N\ge p\ge0$, $q\ge1$, and put $B=N+q+1$. If $V_\rho$ occurs in $\mathcal M_{a,b}$ from <a href="#eq:signed-sector-decomposition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:signed-sector-decomposition">[eq:signed-sector-decomposition]</a>, then $\rho_{q+1}\le q$ and $$e^{F(a,b)}B^{-7q^2}\le D_\rho, \qquad \dim\mathcal M_{a,b}\le e^{F(a,b)}. \label{eq:hook-dimension-explicit}$$ The multiplicity of any $V_\rho$ in $V^{\otimes p}$ is at most $B^{10q^2}$. The occurring shapes are exactly the $(q,q)$-hook shapes. There are at most $(p+1)^{2q}$ such shapes.
  - Explanation: Constituents of a signed sector are exactly hook shapes, with dimensions and multiplicities controlled by the entropy.
- **claim-local-stabilizer-trace** (bound, [source](#stmt-s-local-stabilizer-trace)): Suppose $x\succeq0$ is in $\mathbb C[S_t]$, $h$ specified points are fixed, and $a,b$ each have at most $q$ parts and total size $t-h$. Fix an arbitrary real scalar $G$. Assume, for every $\mu\vdash t$ extending a constituent of $\mathcal M_{a,b}$, $$D_\mu\operatorname{Tr}_\mu x \le \exp\{\eta F(a,b)+G\},\qquad 0\le\eta\le1.$$ Write $\mathsf p(t)$ for the number of partitions of $t$, and let $B=N+q+1$ with $N\ge t$. Then $$\operatorname{Tr}(Q_{a,b}E_{S_{t-h}}x) \le \frac{\mathsf p(t)}{(t)_h} B^{15q^2}\exp\{-(1-\eta)F(a,b)+G\}. \label{eq:local-stabilizer-trace-explicit}$$
  - Explanation: A local trace on a pointwise stabilizer is bounded by a falling-factorial reciprocal times the entropy factor of the type.
- **claim-dimension-range** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-spin-dimension-range)): There is an absolute $c>0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $n$, if $\lambda\vdash n$ has at most $n/2$ rows and $k=n-\lambda_1\ge1$, then $$\log D_\lambda\ge \begin{cases} c k\log(n/k),&k\le n/4,\\ c n,&k\ge n/4. \end{cases}$$
  - Explanation: Shapes with few rows have logarithmic dimension bounded below in terms of the first-row defect.
- **claim-zero-hole** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-spin-zero-hole)): There is an absolute $C_0$ such that, for all sufficiently large $n$, every $\lambda\vdash n$ with at most $n/2$ rows has an occurrence <a href="#ten:spin:eq:1" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ten:spin:eq:1">[ten:spin:eq:1]</a> with $l=0$ and $$F(\alpha,\beta)\le C_0\log D_\lambda. \label{ten:spin:eq:4}$$
  - Explanation: Each shape with at most half as many rows as cards has a hole-free occurrence whose entropy is controlled by its log dimension.
- **claim-decision-forest** (construction, [source](#stmt-cor-decision-forest)): For $n=2^d$ there is a decision forest whose output is always a permutation of $[n]$, whose input consists of independent fair bits, and whose coordinate trees have depth $O(\log n)$, such that the output law has total variation distance $o(1)$ from uniform as $d\to\infty$. The same conclusion holds with independent uniform $[n]$-valued input cells.
  - Explanation: A decision forest with logarithmic-depth coordinate trees over shared fair bits outputs a permutation close to uniform.
- **claim-isotypic-density** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-signed-isotypic-density)): Let $P_\rho$ be the symmetric-group isotypic projection of shape $\rho\vdash p$ on the signed tensor power $V^{\otimes p}$. There is a probability distribution on even densities such that $$P_\rho\preceq D_\rho B^{10q^2}\mathbb E R^{\otimes p}, \qquad B=N+q+1,\quad N\ge p. \label{eq:signed-isotypic-density}$$ If $P_\rho=0$, the assertion is read with any density distribution.
  - Explanation: A full symmetric-group isotypic projection on signed tensors is dominated by a mixture of even-density tensor powers.
- **claim-isotypic-angle** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-signed-isotypic-angle)): On the board $W$ of Theorem <a href="#thm:one-sided-type-angle" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:one-sided-type-angle">9</a>, let $P_\gamma$ be a full $S_p$-isotypic projection, and let $P_R,P_C$ be products of full symmetric-group isotypic projections on the occupied row and column sites. Write their diagrams as $\rho_i$ and $\sigma_j$, and set $D_R=\prod_iD_{\rho_i}$, $D_C=\prod_jD_{\sigma_j}$. With $N=sm$ and $B=N+q+1$, $$\\|P_C P_\gamma P_R\\|_{\rm op}^2 \le\min\left\{1, \frac{D_RD_C}{D_\gamma}B^{10(s+m)q^2}e^l\right\}. \label{eq:signed-isotypic-angle}$$ Empty blocks use the trivial partition and dimension one. The conclusion is uniform over the location of the $l$ missing cells.
  - Explanation: The angle between full row and column isotypic projections interleaved with a global one is bounded by dimension ratios and a missing-cell factor.
- **claim-marked-overlap** (bound, [source](#stmt-s-one-sided-marked-overlap)): Let $W$ contain $n-t$ occupied cells in an $a$ by $b$ board, $n=ab$. Assume $\omega\vdash n-t$ and every prescribed row and column type $\eta_g$ are $(q,q)$-hook shapes: their $(q+1)$st part is at most $q$. Here $1\le q\le n$. Suppose $X=\prod_{g\text{ row}}X_g$ and $Y=\prod_{g\text{ column}}Y_g$ are positive group-algebra elements, with each $X_g,Y_g$ supported on the indicated local type. Write $c_g$ for its unnormalized trace on one copy of $V_{\eta_g}$ and $S=\sum_g\log D_{\eta_g}$. Then $$\operatorname{Tr}_{\omega}(XY) \le\Bigl(\prod_g c_g\Bigr) \exp\{C(a+b+1)q^2\log(n+1)+t +\min(0,S-\log D_\omega)\}.$$ The constant is absolute. On the $\omega$-isotypic part of the signed tensor power, the same bound is multiplied by its multiplicity $w_\omega$.
  - Explanation: The trace of a product of positive row and column factors on a hook shape is bounded by local traces times an entropy gain.
- **claim-positive-mixtures** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ten-positive-mixtures)): Let $A,B\succeq0$ and suppose $A\preceq c_A\int A_x\,d\mu(x)$ and $B\preceq c_B\int B_y\,d\nu(y)$, where $c_A,c_B\ge0$, all integrands are positive, and $\mu,\nu$ are probability measures. Then, for every $P$, $$\\|A^{1/2}PB^{1/2}\\|\le\sqrt{c_Ac_B}\sup_{x,y}\\|A_x^{1/2}PB_y^{1/2}\\|.$$
  - Explanation: Domination by mixtures reduces a sandwiched norm to a supremum over mixture components.
- **claim-missing-cells** (bound, [source](#stmt-ten-missing-cells)): Let $\rho_i$ and $\sigma_j$ be trace-one positive matrices indexed by $a$ rows and $b$ columns. Put $\bar\rho=a^{-1}\sum_i\rho_i$ and use its inverse on its support. For a unitary $U$ set $$z_{ij}=\operatorname{Tr}(\bar\rho^{-1/2}\rho_i\bar\rho^{-1/2}U\sigma_jU^*)\ge0.$$ Then $\sum_{i,j}z_{ij}\le ab$. On any $ab-t$ occupied cells their product is at most $e^t$.
  - Explanation: Removing cells from a full product costs at most an exponential in the number removed.
- **claim-coefficient-extraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-s-compact-coefficient-extraction)): Let $K$ be a compact group, let $\rho$ be a finite-dimensional unitary representation on $\mathcal H$, and let $\pi$ be an irreducible unitary representation on $\mathcal U$ of dimension $M$. Identify the $\pi$-isotypic subspace with $\mathcal U\otimes\mathcal M$. For $A\in\operatorname{End}(\mathcal U)$, let $\widetilde A$ act there as $A\otimes I_{\mathcal M}$ and be zero on all other isotypes. With normalized Haar measure, $$\widetilde A=\int_K f_A(g)\rho(g)\,dg, \qquad f_A(g)=M\operatorname{Tr}(A\pi(g)^*),$$ and $$\int_K\|f_A(g)\|\,dg \le\sqrt M\,\\|A\\|_{\mathrm{HS}} \le M\\|A\\|_{\mathrm{op}}.$$ If the type does not occur, $\widetilde A=0$ and the same identity holds.
  - Explanation: One compact isotype operator is a Haar integral of the representation weighted by a matrix coefficient with controlled absolute integral.
- **claim-general-marked-recurrence** (bound, [source](#stmt-s-general-marked-recurrence)): Let $n=ab\ge4$, where $a,b\ge2$ are powers of two and $\|\log_2a-\log_2b\|\le1$. Arrange the positions as $a$ rows of length $b$, so that $B_n=CR$, where $R$ consists of the $a$ independent row sweeps $B_b$ and $C$ consists of the $b$ independent column sweeps $B_a$. Let $p\ge2$ be real and let $q\ge1$ be any integer. For every $\lambda\vdash n$ with positive moment and every $q$-hook subpartition $\omega\subseteq\lambda$, put $t=n-\|\omega\|$. Then $$\begin{aligned} E_n(\lambda;p)+j_n(t) &\le \max_{\mathcal A}\left\{ \sum_g\bigl(E_{m_g}(\xi_g;p)+j_{m_g}(l_g)\bigr) -\left(\sum_g H_{\eta_g}-H_\omega\right)_+ \right\}\notag\\ &\hspace{12mm} +C\left[t+(a+b)(q^2+n^{1/4})\log(n+1)\right]. \label{s:eq:general-marked-recurrence} \end{aligned}$$ Here $g$ ranges over all rows and columns, and $m_g=b$ on a row and $m_g=a$ on a column. The set $\mathcal A$ consists of choices satisfying $$0\le l_g\le m_g,\qquad \sum_{g\text{ row}}l_g=\sum_{g\text{ column}}l_g=t,$$ $$\xi_g\vdash m_g,\quad E_{m_g}(\xi_g;p)>-\infty, \qquad \eta_g\subseteq\xi_g,\quad \|\eta_g\|=m_g-l_g, \quad (\eta_g)_{q+1}\le q.$$ The set $\mathcal A$ is nonempty when the parent moment is positive. The constant $C$ is absolute, independent of $p,q,a,b,\lambda,\omega$.
  - Explanation: A parent moment is bounded by a maximum over child row and column moments with mark entropy and a dimension penalty.
- **claim-inverse-budget-specialization** (bound, [source](#stmt-s-inverse-budget-specialization)): Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href="#s:general-marked-recurrence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="s:general-marked-recurrence">22</a>, let $0\le\theta\le1$, let $c$ be real, and let $\mathcal B_a,\mathcal B_b\ge0$. Suppose that, for $m\in\{a,b\}$, every $\xi\vdash m$ and every $q$-hook $\eta\subseteq\xi$, with $l=m-\|\eta\|$, satisfy $$E_m(\xi;p)\le \mathcal B_m+\theta H_\eta+c\,l\log m-j_m(l). \label{s:eq:inverse-child-budget}$$ Then every prescribed $q$-hook $\omega\subseteq\lambda$, with $t=n-\|\omega\|$, satisfies $$\begin{aligned} E_n(\lambda;p) &\le\theta H_\omega+c\,t\log n-j_n(t)+a\mathcal B_b+b\mathcal B_a\notag\\ &\hspace{9mm}+C\left[t+(a+b)(q^2+n^{1/4})\log(n+1)\right]. \label{s:eq:inverse-budget-specialization} \end{aligned}$$ The constant $C$ does not depend on $p,q,\theta,c,\mathcal B_a,\mathcal B_b$.
  - Explanation: Child budgets of a given linear form pass to the parent under the recurrence.
- **claim-named-main** (bound, [source](#stmt-ten-named-main)): For the constants fixed above, there are absolute choices of $C_0$ and an integer $h\ge1$ such that, for every dyadic $n\ge2$, every $\lambda\vdash n$, and every occurrence shown below with $m=\|\tau\|$, $$\lambda\ \text{in}\ \mu*\nu^{\rm t}*\tau \quad\Longrightarrow\quad W_n(\lambda) \le \exp\{ \beta_n \mathcal S(\mu,\nu)+ R_n(m)\}. \label{ten:named:eq:9}$$
  - Explanation: A fixed square moment is bounded by an entropy budget of the signed partitions plus a clipped remainder cost.
- **claim-lowering-norm** (bound, [source](#stmt-s-lowering-norm)): There are absolute constants $c>0$ and $C$ such that, for every dyadic $n\ge2$ and every $\lambda\vdash n$ with $2\le k=n-\lambda_1\le n/2$, $$\\|B_n\\|_\lambda^2 \le \exp(-c k\log(n/k)+C k),\qquad 2\le k\le n/2 \label{ten:lowering:eq:1}$$ The block with defect one is zero.
  - Explanation: Lowering operators give a contraction of the squared sweep norm in terms of the first-row defect.
- **claim-lowering-main** (bound, [source](#stmt-ten-lowering-main)): For the fixed constants above, there is a family of real powers $p_n\ge8$, bounded uniformly over dyadic $n\ge2$, such that every $\lambda\vdash n$ satisfies $$\label{ten:lowering:eq:6} D_\lambda\operatorname{Tr}_\lambda \|B_n\|^{p_n} \le \exp L_n(\lambda),\qquad L_n(\lambda)=\min_{\substack{\eta\subseteq\lambda\\q_n\text{-hook}}} \{\beta F(\eta)+G_n(n-\|\eta\|)\}.$$
  - Explanation: A bounded real power gives a moment bounded by the best permitted hook subdiagram cost.
- **claim-inherited-hook** (theorem, [source](#stmt-s-lowering-inherited-hook)): Suppose, at a sufficiently large dyadic size $m$, positive numbers $E_\mu$ satisfy $$\log E_\mu\le \beta F(\eta_0)+G_m(m-\|\eta_0\|) \quad\text{for every $q_m$-hook $\eta_0\subseteq\mu$.} \label{ten:lowering:eq:4}$$ Then every subdiagram $\eta\subseteq\mu$, of size $m-l$, satisfies $$\log E_\mu\le\beta F(\eta)+G_m(l). \label{ten:lowering:eq:5}$$ The same conclusion is automatic when $E_\mu=0$, with logarithm $-\infty$.
  - Explanation: A bound proved for thin hooks extends to every subdiagram.
- **claim-compressed-main** (bound, [source](#stmt-ten-compressed-main)): For sufficiently small fixed $b>0$ and then sufficiently small fixed $c>0$, there are a constant $C$ and exponents $p_d\ge2$ with $\sup_{d\ge1}p_d<\infty$ such that, for every $d\ge1$ and every $\lambda\vdash n=2^d$, $$D_\lambda {\rm tr}\|\rho_\lambda(B_n)\|^{p_d}\le \exp\{L_n(\lambda)+C n^{1-c/2}\}. \label{ten:compressed:eq:1}$$
  - Explanation: Moments are bounded by a minimized tag budget with a sublinear allowance.
- **claim-simultaneous-main** (bound, [source](#stmt-ten-simultaneous-main)): There are an absolute threshold $D$ and, after $D$ is fixed, an absolute power of two $p\ge1$ such that the following bounds hold at every level $l\ge0$ and every size $n=2^d$ with $1\le d\le2^lD$. For every $\lambda\vdash n$ with positive trace, (G) holds. For every such $\lambda$ and every $\mu\subseteq\lambda$ with $\mu_{K_n+1}\le K_n$, put $t=n-\|\mu\|$. If $t>0$ and $\log(n/t)\le u\log n$, then (H) holds: $$\begin{array}{ll} \text{(G)} & e_\lambda\le A_l B_\lambda,\\ \text{(H)} & e_\lambda+J(t,n)\le A_l B_\mu+b_l W(t,n). \end{array} \label{ten:simultaneous:eq:8}$$
  - Explanation: One fixed moment satisfies both a dimension bound and a marked hook bound at every level.
- **claim-inverse-bounded-exponent** (bound, [source](#stmt-s-inverse-bounded-exponent)): There are real exponents $p_d\ge2$, with $\sup_{d\ge1}p_d<\infty$, such that for every $d\ge1$ and every $\lambda\vdash n=2^d$, $$\log J_n(\lambda,p_d)\le \begin{cases} G_n(\lambda),&n-\lambda_1>n^{1-\epsilon},\\ 0,&n-\lambda_1\le n^{1-\epsilon}. \end{cases}$$ For all sufficiently large $n$, the second bound strengthens to $\log J_n(\lambda,p_d)\le-10k\log n$ when $1\le k=n-\lambda_1\le n^{1-\epsilon}$.
  - Explanation: Bounded exponents give an inverse-density hook budget at large levels and a sparse bound otherwise.
- **claim-inverse-main** (bound, [source](#stmt-ten-inverse-main)): Say that a diagram fits the $q$-hook if all cells belong to its first $q$ rows or its first $q$ columns. Write $e_m(l)=l\log(m/l)$, with $e_m(0)=0$. Fix $\theta=1/2$, $c_0=1/100$. Consider $n=ab\ge2$, where $a,b$ are powers of two with $\log_2 a,\log_2 b$ differing by at most one, and $q\le n^{1/16}$ a positive integer. Let $p\ge2$ be real and let $\mathcal B_a,\mathcal B_b\ge0$. Suppose that, for each $m\in\{a,b\}$, every diagram $\xi\vdash m$, and every $q$-hook subdiagram $\eta\subseteq\xi$ of size $m-l$, $$\log J_m(\xi,p)\le \mathcal B_m+\theta H_\eta+c_0\log(m)l-e_m(l), \label{ten:inverse:eq:2}$$ using $-\infty$ for log zero. Then for every $\lambda\vdash n$ and every $q$-hook subdiagram $\omega\subseteq\lambda$ of size $g=n-t$, $$\log J_n(\lambda,p) \le \theta H_\omega+c_0\log(n)t-e_n(t)+ a\mathcal B_b+b\mathcal B_a+O\big(t+(a+b)(q^2+n^{1/4})\log(n+1)\big), \label{ten:inverse:eq:3}$$ The implicit constant is absolute.
  - Explanation: A marked-grid recurrence propagates child budgets to a parent with full mark entropy.
- **claim-arbitrary-hook-allowance** (bound, [source](#stmt-s-arbitrary-hook-child-allowance)): There are absolute $C$ and $m_0$ with the following property. Let $m\ge m_0$ be a power of two and let $p\ge2$. Suppose $$E_m(\xi;p)\le G_m(\xi)\quad\hbox{for large-level $\xi$}, \qquad E_m(\xi;p)\le0\quad\hbox{for the other $\xi$}.$$ Then, for every $\xi\vdash m$ and every subpartition $\eta\subseteq\xi$, putting $l=m-\|\eta\|$ gives $$E_m(\xi;p) \le \mathcal B_m+\theta H_\eta+c_0l\log m-j_m(l), \qquad \mathcal B_m=Cm^{1-4\epsilon}\log(m+1). \label{s:eq:arbitrary-hook-child-allowance}$$ This includes every $q$-hook subpartition, for any inherited $q\ge1$. The constants do not depend on $p$ or $q$.
  - Explanation: Child bounds extend to arbitrary inherited subdiagrams at the cost of an additive allowance.
- **claim-deletion-moment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rec-deletion-moment)): There are real exponents $p(d)\ge1$, indexed by integers $d\ge1$, with $\sup_{d\ge1}p(d)<\infty$ such that, for every integer $d\ge1$ and every partition $\lambda\vdash n=2^d$, $$D_\lambda\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\|B_n\|^{2p(d)}\le e^{E_n^+(\lambda)}.$$ There is a fixed integer $d_*\ge1$ above which one may take $$p(d)=(1+d^{-1/2}) \max\{p(\lfloor d/2\rfloor),p(\lceil d/2\rceil)\}.$$ In particular $E_n^+\le aW_\lambda$ yields a fixed negative power of $D_\lambda$ for the sweep norm.
  - Explanation: Bounded real exponents give a dimension-weighted trace controlled by a clipped deletion budget.
- **claim-hybrid-main** (theorem, [source](#stmt-rec-hybrid-main)): There is a fixed positive integer $p$ such that, simultaneously for every integer $d\ge1$ and every partition $\lambda=(n-k,\beta)\vdash n=2^d$ with $\lambda\ne(n)$ and $\lambda'_1\le n/2$, where $k=n-\lambda_1\ge1$, $$\begin{aligned} \log T_n^\lambda(p)&\le k\log n\left(u_d-\Lambda \left(\frac{\log(n/k)}{\log n}\right)^2\right), \label{rec:hybrid-level-budget}\\ \log T_n^\lambda(p)&\le\mathcal D_d(\lambda). \label{rec:hybrid-diagram-budget} \end{aligned}$$ If $\log(n/k)/\log n\ge\epsilon$, the first bound is at most $-k\log n$. For all sufficiently large $d$, in the complementary range, $\mathcal D_d(\lambda)\le\tfrac14\log D_\lambda$.
  - Explanation: A single integer moment satisfies a level budget and a diagram budget simultaneously for nontrivial diagrams of bounded height.

```json
      "conclusion": {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-schatten",

        "source_id": "PM-238.10"
      },
      "evidence": [

        }
      ],

      "explanation": "The Hilbert-Schmidt norm of a power of a nonnormal matrix is bounded through its operator norm and a trace of powers of its positive square.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-degrees",
        "exact_quote": "Uniformly in $n$, $\\sum_{\\lambda\\vdash n}D_\\lambda^{-1}$ is bounded, and $$\\sum_{\\lambda\\ne(n),(1^n)}D_\\lambda^{-1}\\longrightarrow0.$$ If $\\ell=n-\\max(\\lambda_1,\\lambda'_1)$, then $$\\log D_\\lambda\\ge(\\log2)\\sqrt\\ell/2,\n \\qquad \\sup_n\\sum_{\\lambda\\vdash n}D_\\lambda^{-32}<\\infty.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-238.10"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-degrees",
          "exact_quote": "Uniformly in $n$, $\\sum_{\\lambda\\vdash n}D_\\lambda^{-1}$ is bounded, and $$\\sum_{\\lambda\\ne(n),(1^n)}D_\\lambda^{-1}\\longrightarrow0.$$ If $\\ell=n-\\max(\\lambda_1,\\lambda'_1)$, then $$\\log D_\\lambda\\ge(\\log2)\\sqrt\\ell/2,\n \\qquad \\sup_n\\sum_{\\lambda\\vdash n}D_\\lambda^{-32}<\\infty.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-238.10"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Uniformly in $n$, $\\sum_{\\lambda\\vdash n}D_\\lambda^{-1}$ is bounded, and $$\\sum_{\\lambda\\ne(n),(1^n)}D_\\lambda^{-1}\\longrightarrow0.$$ If $\\ell=n-\\max(\\lambda_1,\\lambda'_1)$, then $$\\log D_\\lambda\\ge(\\log2)\\sqrt\\ell/2,\n \\qquad \\sup_n\\sum_{\\lambda\\vdash n}D_\\lambda^{-32}<\\infty.$$",
      "explanation": "Inverse irreducible dimensions are summable uniformly, with the nontrivial part vanishing, and dimensions grow with the distance from a one-row or one-column shape.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

          "source_id": "PM-238.10"

      "id": "claim-degree-sums",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-signed-type-density",
        "exact_quote": "For every such $(a,b)$ there is a probability distribution on even densities $R$ such that $$Q_{a,b}\\preceq e^{F(a,b)}u(a,b)\\,\\mathbb E R^{\\otimes p}.\n \\label{eq:signed-type-density-exact}$$ For every even density $T$ and every type $(a,b)$, $$\\|T^{\\otimes p}Q_{a,b}\\|_{\\rm op}\\le e^{-F(a,b)}.\n \\label{eq:signed-global-density-upper}$$ Moreover $u(a,b)\\le(p+1)^{q(q-1)}$.*\n\n\n*For arbitrary parity dimensions, the upper bound has the following carrier form. Let $E_+=\\mathbb C^{r_+}$ and $E_-=\\mathbb C^{r_-}$, for nonnegative integers $r_+,r_-$, and let $a,b$ have at most $r_+,r_-$ parts, respectively, with $|a|+|b|=p$. Write $\\mathbf S_a(E_+)$ and $\\mathbf S_b(E_-)$ for the ordinary polynomial carriers of highest weights $a,b$. For a positive parity-preserving matrix $T$ on $E_+\\oplus E_-$, let its induced carrier action be $\\pi_{a,b}(T)=\\mathbf S_a(T|_{E_+})\\otimes\\mathbf S_b(T|_{E_-})$. With $F(a,b)$ given by the same entropy formula, $$\\|\\pi_{a,b}(T)\\|_{\\rm op}\n \\le (\\operatorname{Tr}T)^p e^{-F(a,b)}.\n \\label{eq:signed-carrier-density-upper}$$ A zero-dimensional factor has the empty partition and contributes the scalar identity. If $p=0$, then $\\pi_{a,b}(T)=1$ and the right-hand side is interpreted as one, even when $\\operatorname{Tr}T=0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.10"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-signed-type-density",
          "exact_quote": "For every such $(a,b)$ there is a probability distribution on even densities $R$ such that $$Q_{a,b}\\preceq e^{F(a,b)}u(a,b)\\,\\mathbb E R^{\\otimes p}.\n \\label{eq:signed-type-density-exact}$$ For every even density $T$ and every type $(a,b)$, $$\\|T^{\\otimes p}Q_{a,b}\\|_{\\rm op}\\le e^{-F(a,b)}.\n \\label{eq:signed-global-density-upper}$$ Moreover $u(a,b)\\le(p+1)^{q(q-1)}$.*\n\n\n*For arbitrary parity dimensions, the upper bound has the following carrier form. Let $E_+=\\mathbb C^{r_+}$ and $E_-=\\mathbb C^{r_-}$, for nonnegative integers $r_+,r_-$, and let $a,b$ have at most $r_+,r_-$ parts, respectively, with $|a|+|b|=p$. Write $\\mathbf S_a(E_+)$ and $\\mathbf S_b(E_-)$ for the ordinary polynomial carriers of highest weights $a,b$. For a positive parity-preserving matrix $T$ on $E_+\\oplus E_-$, let its induced carrier action be $\\pi_{a,b}(T)=\\mathbf S_a(T|_{E_+})\\otimes\\mathbf S_b(T|_{E_-})$. With $F(a,b)$ given by the same entropy formula, $$\\|\\pi_{a,b}(T)\\|_{\\rm op}\n \\le (\\operatorname{Tr}T)^p e^{-F(a,b)}.\n \\label{eq:signed-carrier-density-upper}$$ A zero-dimensional factor has the empty partition and contributes the scalar identity. If $p=0$, then $\\pi_{a,b}(T)=1$ and the right-hand side is interpreted as one, even when $\\operatorname{Tr}T=0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.10"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every such $(a,b)$ there is a probability distribution on even densities $R$ such that $$Q_{a,b}\\preceq e^{F(a,b)}u(a,b)\\,\\mathbb E R^{\\otimes p}.\n \\label{eq:signed-type-density-exact}$$ For every even density $T$ and every type $(a,b)$, $$\\|T^{\\otimes p}Q_{a,b}\\|_{\\rm op}\\le e^{-F(a,b)}.\n \\label{eq:signed-global-density-upper}$$ Moreover $u(a,b)\\le(p+1)^{q(q-1)}$.*\n\n\n*For arbitrary parity dimensions, the upper bound has the following carrier form. Let $E_+=\\mathbb C^{r_+}$ and $E_-=\\mathbb C^{r_-}$, for nonnegative integers $r_+,r_-$, and let $a,b$ have at most $r_+,r_-$ parts, respectively, with $|a|+|b|=p$. Write $\\mathbf S_a(E_+)$ and $\\mathbf S_b(E_-)$ for the ordinary polynomial carriers of highest weights $a,b$. For a positive parity-preserving matrix $T$ on $E_+\\oplus E_-$, let its induced carrier action be $\\pi_{a,b}(T)=\\mathbf S_a(T|_{E_+})\\otimes\\mathbf S_b(T|_{E_-})$. With $F(a,b)$ given by the same entropy formula, $$\\|\\pi_{a,b}(T)\\|_{\\rm op}\n \\le (\\operatorname{Tr}T)^p e^{-F(a,b)}.\n \\label{eq:signed-carrier-density-upper}$$ A zero-dimensional factor has the empty partition and contributes the scalar identity. If $p=0$, then $\\pi_{a,b}(T)=1$ and the right-hand side is interpreted as one, even when $\\operatorname{Tr}T=0$.",
      "explanation": "A signed type projection is dominated by an average of tensor powers of even densities, with an entropy and carrier-dimension factor, plus a matching operator-norm bound.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-signed-type-density",
          "exact_quote": "For every such $(a,b)$ there is a probability distribution on even densities $R$ such that $$Q_{a,b}\\preceq e^{F(a,b)}u(a,b)\\,\\mathbb E R^{\\otimes p}.\n \\label{eq:signed-type-density-exact}$$ For every even density $T$ and every type $(a,b)$, $$\\|T^{\\otimes p}Q_{a,b}\\|_{\\rm op}\\le e^{-F(a,b)}.\n \\label{eq:signed-global-density-upper}$$ Moreover $u(a,b)\\le(p+1)^{q(q-1)}$.*\n\n\n*For arbitrary parity dimensions, the upper bound has the following carrier form.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.10"
        }

          "source_id": "PM-238.10"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-one-sided-type-angle",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be any set of $p=sm-l$ occupied cells of an $s$-row, $m$-column rectangle. On $V^{\\otimes W}$, let $P$ be the global $G_q$-type projection with type $(\\alpha,\\beta)$, and let $P_H$ and $P_K$ be products of prescribed column and row $G_q$-type projections. Write $(a^j,b^j)$ for the column types and $(c^i,d^i)$ for the row types, with sizes equal to their respective occupied block sizes. Put $$\\begin{gathered}\n F=F(\\alpha,\\beta),\\qquad F_H=\\sum_jF(a^j,b^j),\\qquad\n F_K=\\sum_iF(c^i,d^i),\\\\\n U_H=\\prod_ju(a^j,b^j),\\qquad U_K=\\prod_iu(c^i,d^i).\n \\end{gathered}$$ If $b_i$ cells are missing from row $i$, put $$\\Phi_K(W)=\\prod_{i:b_i<m}\n       \\left(\\frac m{m-b_i}\\right)^{m-b_i},$$ using factor one when a row is empty. Then $$\\|P_H P_KP\\|_{\\rm op}^2\n \\le\\min\\{1,e^{F_H+F_K-F}U_HU_K\\Phi_K(W)\\}\n \\le\\min\\{1,e^{F_H+F_K-F+l}U_HU_K\\}.\n \\label{eq:one-sided-type-angle-exact}$$ In particular $U_HU_K\\le(p+1)^{(s+m)q(q-1)}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.10"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-one-sided-type-angle",
          "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be any set of $p=sm-l$ occupied cells of an $s$-row, $m$-column rectangle. On $V^{\\otimes W}$, let $P$ be the global $G_q$-type projection with type $(\\alpha,\\beta)$, and let $P_H$ and $P_K$ be products of prescribed column and row $G_q$-type projections. Write $(a^j,b^j)$ for the column types and $(c^i,d^i)$ for the row types, with sizes equal to their respective occupied block sizes. Put $$\\begin{gathered}\n F=F(\\alpha,\\beta),\\qquad F_H=\\sum_jF(a^j,b^j),\\qquad\n F_K=\\sum_iF(c^i,d^i),\\\\\n U_H=\\prod_ju(a^j,b^j),\\qquad U_K=\\prod_iu(c^i,d^i).\n \\end{gathered}$$ If $b_i$ cells are missing from row $i$, put $$\\Phi_K(W)=\\prod_{i:b_i<m}\n       \\left(\\frac m{m-b_i}\\right)^{m-b_i},$$ using factor one when a row is empty. Then $$\\|P_H P_KP\\|_{\\rm op}^2\n \\le\\min\\{1,e^{F_H+F_K-F}U_HU_K\\Phi_K(W)\\}\n \\le\\min\\{1,e^{F_H+F_K-F+l}U_HU_K\\}.\n \\label{eq:one-sided-type-angle-exact}$$ In particular $U_HU_K\\le(p+1)^{(s+m)q(q-1)}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.10"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $W$ be any set of $p=sm-l$ occupied cells of an $s$-row, $m$-column rectangle. On $V^{\\otimes W}$, let $P$ be the global $G_q$-type projection with type $(\\alpha,\\beta)$, and let $P_H$ and $P_K$ be products of prescribed column and row $G_q$-type projections. Write $(a^j,b^j)$ for the column types and $(c^i,d^i)$ for the row types, with sizes equal to their respective occupied block sizes. Put $$\\begin{gathered}\n F=F(\\alpha,\\beta),\\qquad F_H=\\sum_jF(a^j,b^j),\\qquad\n F_K=\\sum_iF(c^i,d^i),\\\\\n U_H=\\prod_ju(a^j,b^j),\\qquad U_K=\\prod_iu(c^i,d^i).\n \\end{gathered}$$ If $b_i$ cells are missing from row $i$, put $$\\Phi_K(W)=\\prod_{i:b_i<m}\n       \\left(\\frac m{m-b_i}\\right)^{m-b_i},$$ using factor one when a row is empty. Then $$\\|P_H P_KP\\|_{\\rm op}^2\n \\le\\min\\{1,e^{F_H+F_K-F}U_HU_K\\Phi_K(W)\\}\n \\le\\min\\{1,e^{F_H+F_K-F+l}U_HU_K\\}.\n \\label{eq:one-sided-type-angle-exact}$$ In particular $U_HU_K\\le(p+1)^{(s+m)q(q-1)}$.",
      "explanation": "The squared norm of the product of column, row and global type projections on a board with missing cells is controlled by entropy differences and a missing-cell factor.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-one-sided-type-angle",
          "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be any set of $p=sm-l$ occupied cells of an $s$-row, $m$-column rectangle.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.10"
        }
      ],
      "id": "claim-one-sided-angle",
      "kind": "theorem",
      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-one-sided-type-angle",
          "exact_quote": "Let $W$ be any set of $p=sm-l$ occupied cells of an $s$-row, $m$-column rectangle.",

      "statement_anchor": "stmt-thm-one-sided-type-angle"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-pointwise-positive-restriction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be finite, let $H\\le G$, and let $x=\\sum_{g\\in G}x_g g$ be positive in $\\mathbb C[G]$. Set $E_Hx=\\sum_{h\\in H}x_hh$. For $\\rho\\in\\widehat H$ let $\\Lambda_\\rho$ be the set of $\\mu\\in\\widehat G$ whose restriction to $H$ contains $\\rho$. With $d_\\rho=\\dim\\rho$ and $d_\\mu=\\dim\\mu$, $$0\\le\\operatorname{Tr}_\\rho(E_Hx)\n \\le\\frac1{[G:H]d_\\rho}\n       \\sum_{\\mu\\in\\Lambda_\\rho}d_\\mu\\operatorname{Tr}_\\mu(x).\n \\label{eq:pointwise-positive-restriction}$$ For $G=S_s$ and $H=S_{s-h}$ fixing $h$ specified points, the index is $(s)_h=s!/(s-h)!$.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.10"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-pointwise-positive-restriction",
          "exact_quote": "Let $G$ be finite, let $H\\le G$, and let $x=\\sum_{g\\in G}x_g g$ be positive in $\\mathbb C[G]$. Set $E_Hx=\\sum_{h\\in H}x_hh$. For $\\rho\\in\\widehat H$ let $\\Lambda_\\rho$ be the set of $\\mu\\in\\widehat G$ whose restriction to $H$ contains $\\rho$. With $d_\\rho=\\dim\\rho$ and $d_\\mu=\\dim\\mu$, $$0\\le\\operatorname{Tr}_\\rho(E_Hx)\n \\le\\frac1{[G:H]d_\\rho}\n       \\sum_{\\mu\\in\\Lambda_\\rho}d_\\mu\\operatorname{Tr}_\\mu(x).\n \\label{eq:pointwise-positive-restriction}$$ For $G=S_s$ and $H=S_{s-h}$ fixing $h$ specified points, the index is $(s)_h=s!/(s-h)!$.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.10"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $G$ be finite, let $H\\le G$, and let $x=\\sum_{g\\in G}x_g g$ be positive in $\\mathbb C[G]$. Set $E_Hx=\\sum_{h\\in H}x_hh$. For $\\rho\\in\\widehat H$ let $\\Lambda_\\rho$ be the set of $\\mu\\in\\widehat G$ whose restriction to $H$ contains $\\rho$. With $d_\\rho=\\dim\\rho$ and $d_\\mu=\\dim\\mu$, $$0\\le\\operatorname{Tr}_\\rho(E_Hx)\n \\le\\frac1{[G:H]d_\\rho}\n       \\sum_{\\mu\\in\\Lambda_\\rho}d_\\mu\\operatorname{Tr}_\\mu(x).\n \\label{eq:pointwise-positive-restriction}$$ For $G=S_s$ and $H=S_{s-h}$ fixing $h$ specified points, the index is $(s)_h=s!/(s-h)!$.",
      "explanation": "Restricting a positive group-algebra element to a subgroup keeps positivity and bounds the trace by the index-scaled ambient traces.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-pointwise-positive-restriction",

      "id": "claim-positive-restriction",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-s-local-stabilizer-trace",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $x\\succeq0$ is in $\\mathbb C[S_t]$, $h$ specified points are fixed, and $a,b$ each have at most $q$ parts and total size $t-h$. Fix an arbitrary real scalar $G$. Assume, for every $\\mu\\vdash t$ extending a constituent of $\\mathcal M_{a,b}$, $$D_\\mu\\operatorname{Tr}_\\mu x\n \\le \\exp\\{\\eta F(a,b)+G\\},\\qquad 0\\le\\eta\\le1.$$ Write $\\mathsf p(t)$ for the number of partitions of $t$, and let $B=N+q+1$ with $N\\ge t$. Then $$\\operatorname{Tr}(Q_{a,b}E_{S_{t-h}}x)\n \\le \\frac{\\mathsf p(t)}{(t)_h}\n    B^{15q^2}\\exp\\{-(1-\\eta)F(a,b)+G\\}.\n \\label{eq:local-stabilizer-trace-explicit}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-238.10"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-s-one-sided-marked-overlap",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W$ contain $n-t$ occupied cells in an $a$ by $b$ board, $n=ab$. Assume $\\omega\\vdash n-t$ and every prescribed row and column type $\\eta_g$ are $(q,q)$-hook shapes: their $(q+1)$st part is at most $q$. Here $1\\le q\\le n$. Suppose $X=\\prod_{g\\text{ row}}X_g$ and $Y=\\prod_{g\\text{ column}}Y_g$ are positive group-algebra elements, with each $X_g,Y_g$ supported on the indicated local type. Write $c_g$ for its unnormalized trace on one copy of $V_{\\eta_g}$ and $S=\\sum_g\\log D_{\\eta_g}$. Then $$\\operatorname{Tr}_{\\omega}(XY)\n \\le\\Bigl(\\prod_g c_g\\Bigr)\n \\exp\\{C(a+b+1)q^2\\log(n+1)+t\n                  +\\min(0,S-\\log D_\\omega)\\}.$$ The constant is absolute. On the $\\omega$-isotypic part of the signed tensor power, the same bound is multiplied by its multiplicity $w_\\omega$.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.10"
      },

Write $c_g$ for its unnormalized trace on one copy of $V_{\\eta_g}$ and $S=\\sum_g\\log D_{\\eta_g}$.         "anchor": "stmt-ten-missing-cells",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\rho_i$ and $\\sigma_j$ be trace-one positive matrices indexed by $a$ rows and $b$ columns. Put $\\bar\\rho=a^{-1}\\sum_i\\rho_i$ and use its inverse on its support.       },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-s-compact-coefficient-extraction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $K$ be a compact group, let $\\rho$ be a finite-dimensional unitary representation on $\\mathcal H$, and let $\\pi$ be an irreducible unitary representation on $\\mathcal U$ of dimension $M$. Identify the $\\pi$-isotypic subspace with $\\mathcal U\\otimes\\mathcal M$. For $A\\in\\operatorname{End}(\\mathcal U)$, let $\\widetilde A$ act there as $A\\otimes I_{\\mathcal M}$ and be zero on all other isotypes. With normalized Haar measure, $$\\widetilde A=\\int_K f_A(g)\\rho(g)\\,dg,\n \\qquad f_A(g)=M\\operatorname{Tr}(A\\pi(g)^*),$$ and $$\\int_K|f_A(g)|\\,dg\n \\le\\sqrt M\\,\\|A\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}\n \\le M\\|A\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}.$$ If the type does not occur, $\\widetilde A=0$ and the same identity holds.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.10"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-s-compact-coefficient-extraction",

For $A\\in\\operatorname{End}(\\mathcal U)$, let $\\widetilde A$ act there as $A\\otimes I_{\\mathcal M}$ and be zero on all other isotypes. With normalized Haar measure, $$\\widetilde A=\\int_K f_A(g)\\rho(g)\\,dg,\n \\qquad f_A(g)=M\\operatorname{Tr}(A\\pi(g)^*),$$ and $$\\int_K|f_A(g)|\\,dg\n \\le\\sqrt M\\,\\|A\\|_{\\mathrm{HS}}\n \\le M\\|A\\|_{\\mathrm{op}}.$$ If the type does not occur, $\\widetilde A=0$ and the same identity holds.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.10"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-s-general-marked-recurrence",
        "exact_quote": "Let $n=ab\\ge4$, where $a,b\\ge2$ are powers of two and $|\\log_2a-\\log_2b|\\le1$. Arrange the positions as $a$ rows of length $b$, so that $B_n=CR$, where $R$ consists of the $a$ independent row sweeps $B_b$ and $C$ consists of the $b$ independent column sweeps $B_a$. Let $p\\ge2$ be real and let $q\\ge1$ be any integer. For every $\\lambda\\vdash n$ with positive moment and every $q$-hook subpartition $\\omega\\subseteq\\lambda$, put $t=n-|\\omega|$. Then $$\\begin{aligned}\n E_n(\\lambda;p)+j_n(t)\n &\\le \\max_{\\mathcal A}\\left\\{\n    \\sum_g\\bigl(E_{m_g}(\\xi_g;p)+j_{m_g}(l_g)\\bigr)\n      -\\left(\\sum_g H_{\\eta_g}-H_\\omega\\right)_+\n                         \\right\\}\\notag\\\\\n &\\hspace{12mm}\n    +C\\left[t+(a+b)(q^2+n^{1/4})\\log(n+1)\\right].\n \\label{s:eq:general-marked-recurrence}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Here $g$ ranges over all rows and columns, and $m_g=b$ on a row and $m_g=a$ on a column. The set $\\mathcal A$ consists of choices satisfying $$0\\le l_g\\le m_g,\\qquad\n \\sum_{g\\text{ row}}l_g=\\sum_{g\\text{ column}}l_g=t,$$ $$\\xi_g\\vdash m_g,\\quad E_{m_g}(\\xi_g;p)>-\\infty,\n \\qquad \\eta_g\\subseteq\\xi_g,\\quad |\\eta_g|=m_g-l_g,\n \\quad (\\eta_g)_{q+1}\\le q.$$ The set $\\mathcal A$ is nonempty when the parent moment is positive. The constant $C$ is absolute, independent of $p,q,a,b,\\lambda,\\omega$.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.10"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

The constant $C$ is absolute, independent of $p,q,a,b,\\lambda,\\omega$.",

        "anchor": "stmt-s-inverse-budget-specialization",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#s:general-marked-recurrence\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"s:general-marked-recurrence\">22</a>, let $0\\le\\theta\\le1$, let $c$ be real, and let $\\mathcal B_a,\\mathcal B_b\\ge0$. Suppose that, for $m\\in\\{a,b\\}$, every $\\xi\\vdash m$ and every $q$-hook $\\eta\\subseteq\\xi$, with $l=m-|\\eta|$, satisfy $$E_m(\\xi;p)\\le \\mathcal B_m+\\theta H_\\eta+c\\,l\\log m-j_m(l).\n \\label{s:eq:inverse-child-budget}$$ Then every prescribed $q$-hook $\\omega\\subseteq\\lambda$, with $t=n-|\\omega|$, satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n E_n(\\lambda;p)\n &\\le\\theta H_\\omega+c\\,t\\log n-j_n(t)+a\\mathcal B_b+b\\mathcal B_a\\notag\\\\\n &\\hspace{9mm}+C\\left[t+(a+b)(q^2+n^{1/4})\\log(n+1)\\right].\n \\label{s:eq:inverse-budget-specialization}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The constant $C$ does not depend on $p,q,\\theta,c,\\mathcal B_a,\\mathcal B_b$.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.10"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-s-inverse-budget-specialization",
          "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#s:general-marked-recurrence\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"s:general-marked-recurrence\">22</a>, let $0\\le\\theta\\le1$, let $c$ be real, and let $\\mathcal B_a,\\mathcal B_b\\ge0$.       "exact_statement": "Under the hypotheses of Proposition <a href=\"#s:general-marked-recurrence\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"s:general-marked-recurrence\">22</a>, let $0\\le\\theta\\le1$, let $c$ be real, and let $\\mathcal B_a,\\mathcal B_b\\ge0$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ten-named-main",
        "exact_quote": "For the constants fixed above, there are absolute choices of $C_0$ and an integer $h\\ge1$ such that, for every dyadic $n\\ge2$, every $\\lambda\\vdash n$, and every occurrence shown below with $m=|\\tau|$, $$\\lambda\\ \\text{in}\\ \\mu*\\nu^{\\rm t}*\\tau\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n W_n(\\lambda)\n \\le\n \\exp\\{ \\beta_n \\mathcal S(\\mu,\\nu)+ R_n(m)\\}.\n \\label{ten:named:eq:9}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-238.10"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-s-lowering-norm",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $c>0$ and $C$ such that, for every dyadic $n\\ge2$ and every $\\lambda\\vdash n$ with $2\\le k=n-\\lambda_1\\le n/2$, $$\\|B_n\\|_\\lambda^2 \\le \\exp(-c k\\log(n/k)+C k),\\qquad\n                         2\\le k\\le n/2                     \\label{ten:lowering:eq:1}$$ The block with defect one is zero.",

          "source_id": "PM-238.10"

      "quantifiers": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-s-lowering-norm",
          "exact_quote": "There are absolute constants $c>0$ and $C$ such that, for every dyadic $n\\ge2$ and every $\\lambda\\vdash n$ with $2\\le k=n-\\lambda_1\\le n/2$, $$\\|B_n\\|_\\lambda^2 \\le \\exp(-c k\\log(n/k)+C k),\\qquad\n                         2\\le k\\le n/2                     \\label{ten:lowering:eq:1}$$ The block with defect one is zero.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.10"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-s-lowering-norm"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ten-lowering-main",
        "exact_quote": "For the fixed constants above, there is a family of real powers $p_n\\ge8$, bounded uniformly over dyadic $n\\ge2$, such that every $\\lambda\\vdash n$ satisfies $$\\label{ten:lowering:eq:6}\n D_\\lambda\\operatorname{Tr}_\\lambda |B_n|^{p_n}\n \\le \\exp L_n(\\lambda),\\qquad\n L_n(\\lambda)=\\min_{\\substack{\\eta\\subseteq\\lambda\\\\q_n\\text{-hook}}}\n             \\{\\beta F(\\eta)+G_n(n-|\\eta|)\\}.$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-s-lowering-inherited-hook",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose, at a sufficiently large dyadic size $m$, positive numbers $E_\\mu$ satisfy $$\\log E_\\mu\\le \\beta F(\\eta_0)+G_m(m-|\\eta_0|)\n \\quad\\text{for every $q_m$-hook $\\eta_0\\subseteq\\mu$.}\n \\label{ten:lowering:eq:4}$$ Then every subdiagram $\\eta\\subseteq\\mu$, of size $m-l$, satisfies $$\\log E_\\mu\\le\\beta F(\\eta)+G_m(l).\n                                                                  \\label{ten:lowering:eq:5}$$ The same conclusion is automatic when $E_\\mu=0$, with logarithm $-\\infty$.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.10"
      },

        "exact_quote": "For sufficiently small fixed $b>0$ and then sufficiently small fixed $c>0$, there are a constant $C$ and exponents $p_d\\ge2$ with $\\sup_{d\\ge1}p_d<\\infty$ such that, for every $d\\ge1$ and every $\\lambda\\vdash n=2^d$, $$D_\\lambda {\\rm tr}|\\rho_\\lambda(B_n)|^{p_d}\\le\n \\exp\\{L_n(\\lambda)+C n^{1-c/2}\\}.\n \\label{ten:compressed:eq:1}$$",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ten-simultaneous-main",
        "exact_quote": "There are an absolute threshold $D$ and, after $D$ is fixed, an absolute power of two $p\\ge1$ such that the following bounds hold at every level $l\\ge0$ and every size $n=2^d$ with $1\\le d\\le2^lD$.         "source_id": "PM-238.10"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ten-inverse-main",
        "exact_quote": "Say that a diagram fits the $q$-hook if all cells belong to its first $q$ rows or its first $q$ columns. Write $e_m(l)=l\\log(m/l)$, with $e_m(0)=0$. Fix $\\theta=1/2$, $c_0=1/100$. Consider $n=ab\\ge2$, where $a,b$ are powers of two with $\\log_2 a,\\log_2 b$ differing by at most one, and $q\\le n^{1/16}$ a positive integer. Let $p\\ge2$ be real and let $\\mathcal B_a,\\mathcal B_b\\ge0$. Suppose that, for each $m\\in\\{a,b\\}$, every diagram $\\xi\\vdash m$, and every $q$-hook subdiagram $\\eta\\subseteq\\xi$ of size $m-l$, $$\\log J_m(\\xi,p)\\le \\mathcal B_m+\\theta H_\\eta+c_0\\log(m)l-e_m(l),\n \\label{ten:inverse:eq:2}$$ using $-\\infty$ for log zero. Then for every $\\lambda\\vdash n$ and every $q$-hook subdiagram $\\omega\\subseteq\\lambda$ of size $g=n-t$, $$\\log J_n(\\lambda,p)\n \\le \\theta H_\\omega+c_0\\log(n)t-e_n(t)+\n a\\mathcal B_b+b\\mathcal B_a+O\\big(t+(a+b)(q^2+n^{1/4})\\log(n+1)\\big),\n \\label{ten:inverse:eq:3}$$ The implicit constant is absolute.",
        "source_id": "PM-238.10"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ten-inverse-main",
          "exact_quote": "Say that a diagram fits the $q$-hook if all cells belong to its first $q$ rows or its first $q$ columns. Write $e_m(l)=l\\log(m/l)$, with $e_m(0)=0$. Let $p\\ge2$ be real and let $\\mathcal B_a,\\mathcal B_b\\ge0$. Suppose that, for each $m\\in\\{a,b\\}$, every diagram $\\xi\\vdash m$, and every $q$-hook subdiagram $\\eta\\subseteq\\xi$ of size $m-l$, $$\\log J_m(\\xi,p)\\le \\mathcal B_m+\\theta H_\\eta+c_0\\log(m)l-e_m(l),\n \\label{ten:inverse:eq:2}$$ using $-\\infty$ for log zero. Then for every $\\lambda\\vdash n$ and every $q$-hook subdiagram $\\omega\\subseteq\\lambda$ of size $g=n-t$, $$\\log J_n(\\lambda,p)\n \\le \\theta H_\\omega+c_0\\log(n)t-e_n(t)+\n a\\mathcal B_b+b\\mathcal B_a+O\\big(t+(a+b)(q^2+n^{1/4})\\log(n+1)\\big),\n \\label{ten:inverse:eq:3}$$ The implicit constant is absolute.",
          "source_id": "PM-238.10"

Then for every $\\lambda\\vdash n$ and every $q$-hook subdiagram $\\omega\\subseteq\\lambda$ of size $g=n-t$, $$\\log J_n(\\lambda,p)\n \\le \\theta H_\\omega+c_0\\log(n)t-e_n(t)+\n a\\mathcal B_b+b\\mathcal B_a+O\\big(t+(a+b)(q^2+n^{1/4})\\log(n+1)\\big),\n \\label{ten:inverse:eq:3}$$ The implicit constant is absolute.",

        "anchor": "stmt-s-arbitrary-hook-child-allowance",
        "exact_quote": "There are absolute $C$ and $m_0$ with the following property. Let $m\\ge m_0$ be a power of two and let $p\\ge2$. Suppose $$E_m(\\xi;p)\\le G_m(\\xi)\\quad\\hbox{for large-level $\\xi$},\n \\qquad\n E_m(\\xi;p)\\le0\\quad\\hbox{for the other $\\xi$}.$$ Then, for every $\\xi\\vdash m$ and every subpartition $\\eta\\subseteq\\xi$, putting $l=m-|\\eta|$ gives $$E_m(\\xi;p)\n \\le \\mathcal B_m+\\theta H_\\eta+c_0l\\log m-j_m(l),\n \\qquad \\mathcal B_m=Cm^{1-4\\epsilon}\\log(m+1).\n \\label{s:eq:arbitrary-hook-child-allowance}$$ This includes every $q$-hook subpartition, for any inherited $q\\ge1$. The constants do not depend on $p$ or $q$.",
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **fair-switch-layer** ([source](#pm-h-10)): A fair switch layer averages the subgroup generated by its disjoint transpositions, and hence is an orthogonal projection $\Pi_i$.
- **even-density** ([source](#pm-h-13)): A density is called even if it is positive, has trace one, and preserves $E$ and $O$.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-annihilation** [claim](#stmt-lem-annihilation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-annihilation): Young's rule on the pair-swap permutation module -> shapes with too many rows vanish
- **step-finite-gap** [claim](#stmt-lem-finitegap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-finitegap): fixed vector under edge transpositions generating the symmetric group -> strict norm gap
- **step-support** [claim](#stmt-ten-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ten-support): counting the coin strings against uniform mass -> lower mixing bound
- **step-signed-density** [claim](#stmt-lem-signed-type-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-signed-type-density): Haar averaging of a highest-weight density -> density domination of types
- **step-angle** [claim](#stmt-thm-one-sided-type-angle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-one-sided-type-angle): mean-density whitening and arithmetic-geometric means -> projection angle estimate
- **step-stabilizer** [claim](#stmt-lem-pointwise-positive-restriction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pointwise-positive-restriction): compression of the regular representation -> positive restriction bound
- **step-zero-hole** [claim](#stmt-lem-spin-zero-hole) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-spin-zero-hole): Durfee square split and hook formula -> occurrence with entropy bounded by log dimension
- **step-spin-moment** [claim](#stmt-s-spin-moment) / [proof](#pm-h-19): induction on a balanced coordinate split with sparse base -> moment bound for every occurrence
- **step-full-deck** [claim](#stmt-cor-spin-full-deck) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-spin-zero-hole-2): Plancherel with inverse degree sums -> fixed-sweep L2 convergence and mixing order
- **step-decision-forest** [claim](#stmt-cor-decision-forest) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-decision-forest): one switch bit per layer, path-following trees -> logarithmic-depth sampler

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-always-permutation**: The decision forest of the corollary is a deterministic function of its switch bits that always outputs a permutation of the n positions: the switch layers compose, for every input, to a permutation.
- **inv-query-depth**: Each coordinate tree follows one card through the layers and queries one switch bit per layer, so its depth is the number of layers of the L sweeps, of order log n.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-keyed-cipher**: Using the forest as a keyed pseudorandom permutation or block cipher. -> The switch bits are independent fair public random bits, one per switch; there is no compact key and no indistinguishability guarantee against adaptive queries.
- **anti-non-dyadic**: Applying the construction directly to a deck whose size is not a power of two. -> The coordinate-layer structure and the bound rely on positions being binary strings of length d.
- **anti-sublinear-shuffles**: Expecting mixing in fewer than order d physical shuffles. -> Support counting prevents mixing below order d shuffles, so a smaller sweep budget leaves the law far from uniform.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The proof of the decision-forest corollary gives an explicit finite procedure: assign one bit per switch of the layers and follow each card's path through them, one query per layer. The reference code implements exactly this path following for given d, L and switch bits.

```python
# INPUTS: one line 'd L bits' with d>=1, L>=1 and bits a 0/1 string of length L*d*2^(d-1)
# PRECONDITIONS: n=2^d; the bit for layer l and switch pair p is bits[l*(n/2)+p]; layer l uses coordinate l mod d
# OUTPUTS: one line with the final position of card 0..n-1 separated by spaces
# TIME: O(n*L*d) bit lookups, each card does L*d adaptive queries
# SPACE: O(n) for the output list
# ARITHMETIC: exact integer shifts and xor, no floating point
# FAILURE: malformed input or too short bit string raises an exception and exits nonzero
import sys

def main():
    t = sys.stdin.read().split()
    d = int(t[0])
    L = int(t[1])
    bits = t[2]
    n = 1 << d
    half = n // 2
    out = []
    for card in range(n):
        x = card
        for l in range(L * d):
            c = l % d
            pair = ((x >> (c + 1)) << c) | (x & ((1 << c) - 1))
            b = bits[l * half + pair]
            if b == "1":
                x ^= 1 << c
        out.append(x)
    print(" ".join(str(v) for v in out))

main()
```

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Corollary 15 gives an explicit per-coordinate evaluation rule over a fixed grid of switch bits, so a library routine can return the image of one card after a fixed number of sweeps. The number of sweeps is only asserted to exist, so a concrete release needs its own calibration. |
| games_simulation | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | The object studied is a card shuffle on a dyadic deck, so a simulator can reuse the sweep structure to produce nearly uniform deals. The guarantee is asymptotic in the deck size, which limits it for small decks. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper supplies analytic bounds, not a procedure for building a checker, converter or analysis tool. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Corollary 15 is a finite construction: card i is traced through a fixed number of layers, and each layer queries one bit selected by the current position. Depth is logarithmic in the deck size, and the output law is within vanishing total-variation distance of uniform. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No dictionary, index or storage layout is proposed; the only stored object is the table of switch bits, which is not a data-structure result. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The layered pair-switch structure resembles a butterfly network, but the paper gives no circuit, area or latency analysis, so no hardware contract can be derived from it. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The input is a large supply of independent fair bits rather than a compact secret key, and no security against adaptive queries is proved, so the construction is not a keyed permutation. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper concerns representation-theoretic bounds for a random walk on the symmetric group; no learning problem, model or training procedure appears. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Budgets and entropy costs in the paper are proof devices for moment bounds, not objectives of an optimisation or scheduling problem. |
| distributed_systems | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | Each coordinate is computed by its own tree reading shared random bits, so different processes can evaluate disjoint outputs independently without materialising the whole permutation, provided they agree on the shared bit table. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | All applicable uses fit the fixed domains; the remaining content is a proof of mixing time for a card shuffle, which is theory rather than an engineering contract. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **l-dyadic-deck-size** (hypothesis): The decision-forest sampler is stated only for deck sizes n=2^d. Its coordinate-layer structure is built on binary positions, so no guarantee is given for other sizes. Blocked use: ['Applying the sampler directly to a 52-card deck or any other non-power-of-two size', 'Reading the logarithmic query depth as a bound for arbitrary array lengths']
- **l-fair-bit-input-model** (hypothesis): The sampler reads independent fair bits (or independent uniform cells), one per switch, shared by all coordinate trees. It is not driven by a compact secret key and carries no security claim. Blocked use: ['Using the forest as a keyed pseudorandom permutation or block cipher', 'Replacing the shared random input by short per-worker seeds and still expecting a permutation close to uniform']
- **l-asymptotic-only-guarantee** (approximation): Convergence is asserted as the deck size tends to infinity, so distance to uniform is only known to vanish in the limit. No explicit finite-size error is given in the statement. Blocked use: ['Claiming exact uniformity of the sampled permutation', 'Quoting a numeric error level for a small concrete deck from this statement alone']
- **l-implicit-existential-constants** (formal_scope): The moment bound holds for sufficiently small fixed constants and an integer power r that depends on them. The size of those constants and of r is not made explicit. Blocked use: ['Fixing a concrete iteration threshold for a small implementation without independent calibration', 'Treating the moment power r as a known numeric value']
- **l-sweep-count-value-absent** (unknown): The numerical value of the absolute number of sweeps that suffices is not given in the source. Blocked use: ['Budgeting a specific number of sweeps from the corollary', 'Comparing the constant of this result with other papers']

- **l-annihilated-shapes** (edge_case): The sign representation and all shapes with more than n/2 rows are annihilated by a sweep. Moment bounds say nothing informative there, because those blocks contribute zero rather than a contraction rate. - **l-support-lower-bound-scope** (formal_scope): Support counting shows that fewer than order d physical shuffles cannot mix, because the law is supported on too few permutations. The order d is therefore also a lower bound for this construction. ### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 238 (`lean/docs/238.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Optimal-order mixing of the Thorp shuffle](../../preprints/Optimal-order-mixing-of-the-Thorp-shuffle-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [From partial permutation information to Fourier bounds](../../preprints/From-partial-permutation-information-to-Fourier-bounds-September-26-2026/main.pdf)
> - [Conditional permutations in a revealed switching environment](../../preprints/Conditional-permutations-in-a-revealed-switching-environment-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Routing densities and representation contraction for Thorp sweeps](../../preprints/Routing-densities-and-representation-contraction-for-Thorp-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Row–column symmetry and contraction of coordinate sweeps](../../preprints/Row-column-symmetry-and-contraction-of-coordinate-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Random-subspace tests and trace smoothing for coordinate sweeps](../../preprints/Random-subspace-tests-and-trace-smoothing-for-coordinate-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Compatibility entropy and the spectrum of a Thorp sweep](../../preprints/Compatibility-entropy-and-the-spectrum-of-a-Thorp-sweep-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Signed tensor densities and diagram budgets for the Thorp shuffle](../../preprints/Signed-tensor-densities-and-diagram-budgets-for-coordinate-sweeps-September-26-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Conditional coordinate sweeps and analytic transfer](../../preprints/Conditional-coordinate-sweeps-and-analytic-transfer-September-26-2026/main.pdf)
> - [A strict four-row permanent inequality and permutation moments](../../preprints/A-strict-four-row-permanent-inequality-and-permutation-moments-September-26-2026/main.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves optimal-order mixing of the Thorp shuffle on $2^d$ cards. After $1600d$ complete shuffles, the full permutation law converges in total variation to uniform as $d\to\infty$, uniformly over initial decks. Together with the support lower bound of $2d-O(1)$, this gives mixing time $\Theta(d)=\Theta(\log(2^d))$.
>
> The linked supporting results include frame and conditional-list estimates, regular trace and spectral bounds, signed moments, and full-density $L^2$ control. They also bound reciprocal Specht-dimension sums and give the eight-block Fourier estimate used in the mixing argument.
>
> The formalized supporting result turns bounds on partial-permutation cosets into Fourier bounds. Partition a finite set into $b\ge1$ nonempty blocks, and let $f\ge0$ be a subprobability weight on its symmetric group whose left-coset masses for each block subgroup are at most $B$. For every irreducible unitary representation of dimension $D$ and every $u>0$, both the squared Hilbert–Schmidt norm and squared operator norm of its Fourier transform are at most $bB\,C(u)D^{-1+(u+2)/b}$, where $C(u)$ is the stated symmetric-group degree constant.
>
> The paper's asymptotic conclusion about the product of two random permutations is outside this selected finite estimate.
>
> The formalized results give averaged conditional mixing for half-permutations in the revealed-path model and full-deck mixing for the Thorp shuffle. In particular, the full-deck total-variation distance tends to zero after $16040400d$ steps on $2^d$ cards, uniformly over deterministic initial decks. The conditional estimate is averaged over the actual outside-path law, rather than asserted for each individual environment. The formalization also includes the associated overlay, reset minorization, and two-color conditional constructions.
>
> The formalization covers nine routing and representation estimates for Thorp sweeps: adaptive operator and fourth-trace bounds, Casimir moments, dense truncation, sparse contact, harmonic-cycle contraction, smoothing, and three tail or sparse-saving estimates. They bound routing-density deviations and Fourier operators in dense and sparse regimes, including exponential savings in the level scale and representation dimension.
>
> The conditional high-height estimate requires a sufficiently large cutoff height $J$ that exceeds twice the number $s$ of coordinates in the fixed low block, so $J>2s$. These are the earlier nine statements associated with the paper; its changed later statements and six other result blocks are outside this scope.
>
> The formalization proves uniform representation bounds for one coordinate sweep of the Thorp shuffle. For each irreducible representation of dimension $D$, it chooses a weight between $D^{3/4}$ and $D$ and a Schatten exponent bounded by one absolute constant so that the weighted Schatten moment is at most one. The sweep's operator norm is consequently at most $D^{-c}$ for one absolute $c>0$.
>
> The supporting row–column estimate bounds the total squared overlap over all multiplicity copies on an occupied board, with explicit dependence on the row and column representation dimensions, the global dimension, the number of missing cells, and the number of rows or columns containing the global Young diagram. The paper's full physical-mixing conclusion is outside these selected representation estimates.
>
> The formalization proves a uniform trace-smoothing estimate for coordinate sweeps of the Thorp shuffle on $2^d$ cards. One absolute sweep parameter works for every $d\ge1$: the regular trace is at most $1+(2^d)^{-10}$, and the full permutation law after the corresponding fixed number of sweeps is within $\tfrac12(2^d)^{-5}$ of uniform in total variation, for every initial deck. Thus the selected upper bound uses $O(d)$ physical shuffles.
>
> The formalized result is the weighted compatibility theorem for balanced $A\times D$ rectangles, with $n=AD$ and $\sqrt n/2\le A,D\le2\sqrt n$. For arbitrary nonnegative weights on the row and column permutation groups, the normalized compatibility average is bounded by $\exp(C_0n^{54/100})$ times the product of the marginal $L^{1/\theta}$ factors, where $\theta=1-L/\log\sqrt n>0$ and the constants are absolute. Compatibility means injectivity in every original column. The bounded regular-moment and other moment/rank conclusions are not included.
>
> The formalization gives signed representation estimates for coordinate sweeps of the Thorp shuffle. For every signed occurrence of a Young diagram of size $2^d$, it bounds the logarithm of a fixed-order weighted sweep moment by a small multiple of the signed partition entropy plus an explicit remainder-size budget. The moment order is uniform over the diagrams and decompositions after the two small coefficients are fixed.
>
> The linked supporting angle bound controls the overlap of row, column, and global signed-type projections on an occupied rectangle, with explicit entropy, dimension, and missing-cell factors. These selected estimates support the paper's full-density argument; the full $L^2$ and total-variation mixing conclusion is outside them.
>
> The formalized binary-sweep theorem gives a universal power contraction in every irreducible representation: for sufficiently large $d$, the averaged sweep on $2^d$ slots has operator norm at most $D^{-g}$ in representation dimension $D$, for some $g>0$. The sign expectation is zero, and a fixed number of independent sweeps approaches uniform in total variation from every initial deck. A separate conditional moment estimate covers allowed power-of-two grids and disjoint coordinate-respecting trajectories, with its stated feasibility premise. Physical-shuffle law identification is not included.
>
> The formalization proves the paper's strict four-row permanent inequality. There are constants $4/3<p<2$ and $\varepsilon>0$ such that every probability law on $S_4$ within total-variation distance $\varepsilon$ of uniform, and with exactly uniform coordinate marginals, satisfies the permanent bound by the product of the four $L^p$ row norms for every nonnegative matrix. The exponent and neighborhood are uniform over those laws. The Thorp mixing consequence is outside this selected statement.
